Durbajlo worked at the [Military University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_University_of_Technology), working on telecommunications security projects.[^cite_note-ReutersJuly2-307] After retiring from the ISA, he began working as a consultant for [Orange Polska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Polska).[^cite_note-ReutersJuly2-307]

#### Russia

In December 2007, Igor Reshetin, the Chief Executive of Tsniimash-Export, and three researchers were sentenced to prison for passing on dual-purpose technology to the Chinese. Analysts speculated that the leaked technology could help China develop improved [missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missiles) and accelerate the [Chinese space program](343674.md).[^cite_note-308] In September 2010, the Russian [Federal Security Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Security_Service_\(Russia\)) detained two scientists working at the [Baltic State Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_State_Technical_University) in [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md). The two are charged with passing on classified information to China, possibly through the [Harbin Engineering University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbin_Engineering_University).[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] Russia has been a significant target for China linked hackers.[^cite_note-Cyberscoop_2020-149]

In February 2020, the [FSB](385990.md) detained Valery Mitko, President of Russia's Arctic Academy of Sciences, on charges of providing classified information related to underwater detection of submarines to Chinese intelligence operatives.[^cite_note-311] In 2022, Mitko died under a house arrest challenged as being extralegal.[^cite_note-312]

In June 2025, *The New York Times* reported that a leaked internal FSB memo raised concerns about [China](5405.md) with respect to [industrial espionage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_espionage) of sensitive Russian technologies.[^cite_note-313] Information on Russia's weaponry has increasingly been targeted by [advanced persistent threats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_persistent_threat) emanating from China.[^cite_note-314]

#### Turkey

China has engaged in espionage campaigns in order to monitor [Uyghurs in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uyghurs_of_Turkey) that have involved coercion and sophisticated cyber-espionage measures.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] In February 2024, Turkey arrested six individuals suspected of spying on Uyghurs in the country on behalf of China's intelligence service.[^cite_note-319] In May 2025, the [Turkish National Intelligence Organization](575957.md) (MIT) reportedly dismantled a Chinese cyber-espionage cell in [Istanbul](3391396.md), accused of using ghost base stations to collect communication data and user information and conduct surveillance of Turkish public officials and Uyghur Turks.[^cite_note-320]

#### Switzerland

According to reports in *[Neue Zürcher Zeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Z%C3%BCrcher_Zeitung)*, Chinese intelligence services have attempted to recruit Swiss university staff and researchers using LinkedIn.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322]

#### Sweden

Babur Maihesuti, a Uyghur who became a Swedish citizen was arrested for spying on the Uyghur refugee communities in Sweden, Norway, Germany and the United States, and ultimately sentenced for illegal espionage activity.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325] In April 2018, Sweden charged Dorjee Gyantsan, a 49-year-old Tibetan refugee, with spying on Tibetan dissidents and refugees in Sweden between July 2015 and February 2017.[^cite_note-nytsweden-326] Gyantsan is accused of collecting information on Tibetan refugees in Sweden, and then passing that information on to Chinese officials in [Finland](10577.md) and [Poland](22936.md).[^cite_note-nytsweden-326] Gyantsan was arrested upon returning from [Warsaw](32908.md), carrying \$6,000 in cash.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

#### United Kingdom

This paragraph is an excerpt from [Chinese espionage in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_espionage_in_the_United_Kingdom).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chinese_espionage_in_the_United_Kingdom\&action=edit)]

The [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_People%E2%80%99s_Republic_of_China) of the [People's Republic of China](5405.md) (PRC) and various organs of the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) (CCP) have conducted surveillance and espionage in the United Kingdom, according to top UK national security officials such as the head of its domestic intelligence agency [MI5](100076.md).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] UK officials, including experts at its MI5, have long been fearful that the PRC could shut down businesses in the nation with [cyberattacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberattacks) and spy equipment embedded in computer and telecommunications equipment.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

#### Vatican City

According to an investigation by [Recorded Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recorded_Future), Chinese hackers broke into the computer networks of the Vatican and the Holy See's Study Mission to China, including by placing malware in what appeared to be a letter from [Cardinal Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State) [Pietro Parolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Parolin).[^cite_note-334] In the weeks after the investigation was published the hackers continued to operate on Vatican networks,[^cite_note-335] by November 2020 the group targeting the Vatican had changed their tactics to avoid detection but were discovered by researchers from the firm Proofpoint.[^cite_note-336]

### North America

#### Canada

Further information: [Chinese government interference in Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_government_interference_in_Canada)

Newspapers have estimated that China may have up to 1,000 spies in Canada.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338] The head of the [Canadian Security Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Security_Intelligence_Service) [Richard Fadden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Fadden) in a television interview was assumed to have implied that various [Canadian politicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_in_Canada) at provincial and municipal levels had ties to Chinese intelligence. In an interview, he claimed that some politicians were under the influence of a foreign government, but he withdrew the statement a few days later. It was assumed by Chinese groups in Canada, and others, that he was referring to China because in the same interview he stressed the high level of Chinese spying in Canada, however Fadden did not say specifically which country these politicians were under the influence of. His statement was withdrawn a few days later.[^cite_note-339]

In 2005, Canadian businessman Joe Wang stated his belief that threatening letters he received after broadcasting programs about alleged human rights abuses in China were from the Chinese consulate; one of the envelopes contained [boric acid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boric_acid).[^cite_note-cbc1-340]

In 2012 [Mark Bourrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Bourrie), an [Ottawa](22219.md)-based freelance journalist, stated that the [State Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Council_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China)-run Xinhua News Agency asked him to collect information on the Dalai Lama through their Ottawa bureau chief, Dacheng Zhang, by exploiting his journalistic access to the [Parliament of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Canada).[^cite_note-341] Bourrie stated that he was asked to write for Xinhua in 2009 and sought advice from the Canadian Security Intelligence Service (CSIS), but was ignored. Bourrie was asked to collect information on the Sixth World Parliamentarians' Convention on Tibet at the [Ottawa Convention Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa_Convention_Centre), although Xinhua had no intention of writing a story on the proceedings. Bourrie stated that at that point "We were there under false pretenses, pretending to be journalists but acting as government agents."[^cite_note-342] Xinhua collects extensive information on [Tibetan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_people) and [Falun Gong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falun_Gong) dissidents in Canada, and is accused of being engaged in espionage by Chinese defector [Chen Yonglin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Yonglin) and [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md).

On 1 December 2013, [Lloyd's Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd's_Register) employee Qing Quentin Huang was arrested and charged with violating the *[Security of Information Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_of_Information_Act)*, for allegedly communicating classified information on the federal shipbuilding strategy to China.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-Bell-344] Huang reportedly contacted the [Chinese Embassy in Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China,_Ottawa) in an attempt to pass on secrets, which was detected by the Canadian Security Intelligence Service, who in turn alerted the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police](69080.md).[^cite_note-Bell-344]

Between 2006 and 2010 Yang Wang, a Chinese immigrant to Canada, admitted to providing intelligence to the Ministry of State Security, including on the activities of [Falun Gong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falun_Gong_outside_mainland_China).[^cite_note-345]

Around June 2014, the [National Research Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Research_Council_\(Canada\)) was reportedly penetrated by Chinese state-sponsored hackers.[^cite_note-forced-346]

In 2022, Yuesheng Wang, a researcher at [Hydro-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydro-Qu%C3%A9bec), was arrested and charged with violating the *Security of Information Act*, for allegedly obtaining [trade secrets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_secrets_in_Canada) and filing patents in connection with Chinese universities and publishing papers without the permission of Hydro-Québec. He is also charged with fraud for obtaining trade secrets, unauthorized use of a computer and breach of trust by a public officer. His crimes were allegedly committed between February 2018 and October 2022.[^cite_note-347]

#### United States

Main articles: [Chinese espionage in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_espionage_in_the_United_States) and [List of Chinese spy cases in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_spy_cases_in_the_United_States)

See also: [1996 United States campaign finance controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_United_States_campaign_finance_controversy)

> I think it's more than likely we're going to end up, if we end up in a war – a real shooting war with a major power – it's going to be as a consequence of a cyber breach of great consequence and it's increasing exponentially, the capabilities.
>
> &#x20;— [President Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Biden), at the [Office of the Director of National Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Director_of_National_Intelligence) on 27 July 2021, in front of the U.S. intelligence community
>
> [^cite_note-348]



[Delta IV Heavy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_IV_Heavy) in 2018



[F-15 Eagle](11715.md) in 2004

China is suspected of having a long history of espionage in the United States against military and industrial secrets, often resorting to the exploitation of commercial entities and a network of scientific, academic, and business contacts. Several U.S. citizens have been convicted for spying for China. Naturalized citizen Dongfan Chung, an engineer working with [Boeing](18933266.md), was the first person convicted under the [Economic Espionage Act of 1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Espionage_Act_of_1996). Chung is suspected of having passed on classified information on designs including the [Delta IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_IV) rocket, [F-15 Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15_Eagle), [B-52 Stratofortress](18933037.md) and the [CH-46](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CH-46) and [CH-47](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CH-47) helicopters.[^cite_note-349]

The [U.S. Department of Justice](52563.md) investigation into the fund-raising activities had uncovered evidence that Chinese agents sought to direct contributions from foreign sources to the [Democratic National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_National_Committee) (DNC) before the [1996 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_United_States_presidential_election). The [Chinese embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China_in_Washington,_D.C.) in Washington, D.C. was used to coordinate contributions to the DNC.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351]

Taiwanese-American scientist [Wen Ho Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wen_Ho_Lee) (born in [Nantou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantou_City), Taiwan 21 December 1939) was accused and investigated on the grounds of espionage in 1999 but was acquitted of all charges except for mishandling classified data.[^cite_note-352]

In November 2005 the United States arrested four people in Los Angeles on suspicion of being involved in a Chinese spy ring.[^cite_note-wash1-353]

In 2008 the Chinese government was accused of secretly copying information from the laptop of Commerce Secretary [Carlos Gutierrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Gutierrez) during a trade mission to Beijing to gain information on American corporations.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355] The allegations were subsequently dismissed by Qin Gang, a spokesman for the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China).[^cite_note-356]

In 2009 China was suspected of stealing terabytes of design data for the [F-35 Joint Strike Fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35_Joint_Strike_Fighter) from defense contractor Lockheed Martin's computers. In 2012, a Chinese version, the [J-31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenyang_FC-31), appeared to rival the F-35.[^cite_note-357]

China's espionage and cyberattacks against the US government and business organizations are a major concern, according to the seventh annual report (issued September 2009) to the [US Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Congress) of the [United States–China Economic and Security Review Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93China_Economic_and_Security_Review_Commission).[^cite_note-358] "Although attribution is a problem in cyber attacks, the scale and coordination of the attacks strongly indicates Chinese state involvement", said commission vice chairman [Larry Wortzel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Wortzel). "In addition to harming U.S. interests, Chinese human and cyber espionage activities provide China with a method for leaping forward in economic, technological, and military development." The report cited that the number of cyberattacks from China against the [US Department of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Department_of_Defense) computer systems had grown from 43,880 in 2007 to 54,640 in 2008, a nearly 20 percent increase.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] Reuters reported that the Commission found that the Chinese government has placed many of its computer network responsibilities under the direction of the People's Liberation Army, and was using the data mostly for military purposes.[^cite_note-361] In response, China slammed the report as "full of prejudice", and warning it could damage China-US relations. "We advise this so-called commission not to always view China through tinted glasses", Foreign Ministry spokesman [Qin Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Gang) said.[^cite_note-362]

In June 2015, the [United States Office of Personnel Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Office_of_Personnel_Management) (OPM) announced that it had been the target of a [data breach](13307577.md) targeting the records of as many as four million people.[^cite_note-363] Later, FBI Director [James Comey](2156690.md) put the number at 18 million.[^cite_note-364] *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* has reported that the attack originated in China, citing unnamed government officials.[^cite_note-365] James Comey said: "It is a very big deal from a national security perspective and from a counterintelligence perspective. It's a treasure trove of information about everybody who has worked for, tried to work for, or works for the United States government."[^cite_note-366]

[Voice of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_America) reported in April 2020 that "U.S. intelligence agencies concluded the Chinese hackers meddled in both the 2016 and 2018 elections" and "Internet security researchers say there have already been signs that China-allied hackers have engaged in so-called 'spear-phishing' attacks on American political targets" ahead of the [2020 United States elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_elections).[^cite_note-367]

In 2019, two Chinese nationals were indicted for the [Anthem medical data breach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthem_medical_data_breach).[^cite_note-368] About 80 million company records were hacked, stoking fears that the copied data could be used for [identity theft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_theft_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-369] In February 2020, the United States government indicted members of China's PLA for hacking into [Equifax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equifax) and plundering sensitive data as part of a massive heist that also included stealing trade secrets.[^cite_note-cbs_china_deny-370][^cite_note-371] Private records of more than 145 million Americans were compromised in the [2017 Equifax data breach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Equifax_data_breach).[^cite_note-breach-372]

In July 2020, FBI Director [Christopher A. Wray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_A._Wray) called China the "greatest long-term threat" to the United States. He said that "the FBI is now opening a new China-related counterintelligence case every 10 hours. Of the nearly 5,000 active counterintelligence cases currently under way across the country, almost half are related to China."[^cite_note-breach-372]

> The greatest long-term threat to our nation’s information and intellectual property, and to our economic vitality, is the counterintelligence and economic espionage threat from China. It’s a threat to our economic security—and by extension, to our national security...It’s the people of the United States who are the victims of what amounts to Chinese theft on a scale so massive that it represents one of the largest transfers of wealth in human history. We’ve now reached the point where the FBI is opening a new China-related counterintelligence case about every 10 hours. Of the nearly 5,000 active FBI counterintelligence cases currently underway across the country, almost half are related to China.
>
> &#x20;— [Director of the FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director_of_the_FBI) [Christopher Wray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_A._Wray), *Remarks at the Hudson Institute (July 7, 2020)*

In July 2020, the United States Department of Justice indicted two Chinese hackers with global computer intrusion campaign targeting intellectual property and confidential business information, including [COVID-19](63030231.md) research. It alleged that the two hackers worked with the Guangdong State Security Department of the Ministry of State Security (China).[^cite_note-373]

In a July 2021 joint statement with NATO, the EU, and other [Western nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_nations), the US accused the Ministry of State Security of perpetrating several cyberattacks, including the 2021 Microsoft Exchange Server data breach. However, it also noted that several attacks were for-profit ransomware attacks by non-government hackers contracted by the MSS for non-intelligence purposes. Additionally, the U.S. Justice Department charged four Chinese nationals accused of working for the MSS with a hacking campaign targeting government, academic, and private institutions; the individuals were each charged with one count of [conspiracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_\(criminal\)) to commit [computer fraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_fraud) and conspiracy to commit [economic espionage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_espionage).[^cite_note-19Jul1-213][^cite_note-19Jul2-214][^cite_note-19Jul3-215][^cite_note-19Jul4-216]

In November 2022, Yanjun Xu, the first Chinese government intelligence officer ever to be extradited to the United States to stand trial was sentenced to 20 years in prison for espionage crimes, attempting to steal trade secrets.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] According to the U.S. Justice Department, Xu targeted American aviation companies, recruited employees to travel to China, and solicited their proprietary information, all on behalf of China.[^cite_note-\:6-376][^cite_note-377]

In March 2023, the Center for Strategic and International Studies (CSIS) based in Washington DC published an updated Survey of Chinese Espionage in the United States since 2000, which includes 224 documented cases of Chinese espionage targeted at the United States as of February 2023.[^cite_note-\:7-378] The list of cases is based on publicly accessible sources and CSIS believes it is likely incomplete.[^cite_note-\:7-378] According to CSIS, Chinese intelligence in the United States surpasses any other nation, including Russia.[^cite_note-\:7-378] While the economic and technological espionage costs the United States billions of dollars since 2000, Chinese espionage has also resulted in immeasurable harm to national security, particularly through the theft of weapon technology, including data related to nuclear weapons testing.[^cite_note-\:7-378][^cite_note-379] CSIS observed that in recent years, China has expanded its espionage efforts to include the theft of substantial amounts of [personal information](1845896.md) (PII), political manipulation, and influence operations.[^cite_note-\:7-378]

On 3 August 2023, the United States Department of Justice announced two indictments of two U.S. Navy servicemembers for transmitting military information to Chinese intelligence officers. Jinchao Wei, an active-duty Navy sailor on the [amphibious assault ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibious_assault_ship), the [U.S.S. Essex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Essex_\(LHD-2\)) stationed at Naval Base San Diego, was indicted for conspiracy to send national defense information to a Chinese spy.[^cite_note-\:8-380] Petty Officer Wenheng Zhao was charged with receiving bribes from a Chinese spy in exchange for classified U.S. military information.[^cite_note-\:8-380] On 8 January 2024, Zhao was convicted of transmitting sensitive U.S. military information to a Chinese intelligence officer in exchange for bribery payments and sentenced to 27 months in prison and ordered to pay a \$5,500 fine.[^cite_note-381]

In January 2025, the United States Department of Justice announced the indictment and arrest of John Harold Rogers, a former senior advisor to the [Federal Reserve Board of Governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Board_of_Governors), on charges that he conspired to steal Federal Reserve trade secrets for the Chinese government.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383]

### Oceania

#### Australia

See also: [2019 Australian Parliament infiltration plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Parliament_infiltration_plot)

Former [Department of Defence Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Defence_\(Australia\)) [Dennis Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Richardson_\(diplomat\)) has stated that China is engaged in extensive espionage against Australia, and included surveillance of [Chinese Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Australian) communities.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-Hamilton2018-385] Australia believes that the Chinese government have been spying on Australian businesses.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] A male Chinese student from [Fujian](88585.md) was granted a protection visa by the Refugee Review Tribunal of Australia after revealing that he had been instructed to spy on Australian targets in exchange for an overseas scholarship, reporting to the Ministry of State Security.[^cite_note-388]

[Nicola Roxon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicola_Roxon), the [Attorney-General of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney-General_of_Australia), blocked the [Shenzhen](83368.md)-based corporation Huawei from seeking a supply contract for the [National Broadband Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Broadband_Network), on the advice of the [Australian Security Intelligence Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Security_Intelligence_Organisation) (ASIO).[^cite_note-389] The Australian government feared Huawei would provide backdoor access for Chinese cyber espionage.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

The Chinese government is suspected of orchestrating an attack on the email network used by the [Parliament of Australia](302298.md), allowing unauthorized access to thousands of emails and compromising the computers of several senior Australian politicians including Prime Minister [Julia Gillard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Gillard), Foreign Minister [Kevin Rudd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Rudd), and [Minister of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_\(Australia\)) [Stephen Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Smith_\(Australian_politician\)).[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393]

[Sheri Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheri_Yan) and a former [Office of National Assessments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_National_Assessments) (ONA) official, [Roger Uren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Uren), were investigated by ASIO on suspicion of spying for China.[^cite_note-ASIO-394] Uren, former Assistant Secretary responsible for the Asia section of ONA, was found to have removed documents pertaining to Chinese intelligence operations in Australia, and kept them in his apartment.[^cite_note-ASIO-394] Yan was suspected of undertaking influence operations on behalf of the Chinese Communist Party, and introducing Colonel [Liu Chaoying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liu_Chaoying), a [military intelligence](146678.md) officer, to Australian contacts.[^cite_note-ASIO-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-AAP-396]

Hackers either working for or on behalf of the government of China are suspected as being responsible for a cyber-espionage attack against an Australian defense company.[^cite_note-Ars2-397][^cite_note-398] Designated APT [Alf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alf_Stewart) by the [Australian Signals Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Signals_Directorate), the hackers stole approximately 30 gigabytes of data on projects including the F-35 Joint Strike Fighter, the [P-8 Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P-8_Poseidon), the [C-130 Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130_Hercules) and the [Joint Direct Attack Munition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Direct_Attack_Munition).[^cite_note-Ars2-397] APT Alf used a remote access tool dubbed "China Chopper".[^cite_note-Ars2-397]

In 2017, Chinese hackers infiltrated the computers of [Australian National University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_National_University), potentially compromising national security research conducted at the university.[^cite_note-ANU-399][^cite_note-400] In 2015, Chinese hackers infiltrated the [Bureau of Meteorology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Meteorology).[^cite_note-ANU-399]

In February 2019, the *[Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sydney_Morning_Herald)* reported that Chinese businessman and real-estate developer [Huang Xiangmo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huang_Xiangmo) had been denied permanent residency by the [Department of Home Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Home_Affairs_\(Australia\)) reportedly due to character and national security concerns.[^cite_note-401] Huang was the chairman of the Australian Council for the Promotion of the Peaceful Reunification of China (ACPPRC), the [China Council for the Promotion of Peaceful National Reunification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Council_for_the_Promotion_of_Peaceful_National_Reunification) (CCPPNR) and the Oceanic Alliance of the Promotion of Peaceful Reunification of China; all three either umbrella organizations of the United Front Work Department or having close ties with the UFWD itself.[^cite_note-402] Prior to his forced departure, Huang had been active in Australian political circles, donating some \$2.7 million to both the [Australian Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party) and [Liberal](18453.md) parties respectively as well as delivering \$100,000 in cash to the New South Wales branch of the Australian Labor party in breach of electoral donation laws.[^cite_note-403] In 2021, Huang was elected to Hong Kong's new electoral committee implemented under the [2021 Hong Kong electoral changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hong_Kong_electoral_changes) imposed on Hong Kong by the National People's Congress in Beijing with the electoral message of "Support\[ing] the implementation of ‘patriots administer\[ing] Hong Kong".[^cite_note-404]

In March 2019, the *[Australian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation)* reported that the body of a member of the Australian Liberal Party Bo ("Nick") Zhao had been inside a hotel room in [Melbourne](17306237.md). Zhao had been a member of the Liberal party in the electorate of [Chisholm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_of_Chisholm), [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(Australia\)) since 2015. Reports emerged afterwards that Zhao had been approached by a businessman originating from [China](5405.md) who offered a \$1 million in exchange for Zhao's running of candidacy to the Federal Parliament.[^cite_note-405] Zhao allegedly reported the encounter to the Australian Security and Intelligence Organisation resulting in his death months later.[^cite_note-\:1-406] Member of the [Parliamentary Joint Intelligence Committee on Intelligence and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_Joint_Committee_on_Intelligence_and_Security) (PJCIS) [Andrew Hastie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Hastie) stated that Zhao was: "the perfect target for cultivation", remarking that he was "a guy who was a bit of a high-roller in Melbourne, living beyond his means, someone who was vulnerable to a foreign state intelligence service cultivating \[him]."[^cite_note-\:1-406]

In April 2023, it was reported that two individuals believed to be Chinese spies provided a Sydney-based businessman with cash-filled envelopes in exchange for intelligence on various topics, including an Australian government agreement involving the construction of Australian [nuclear-powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_submarine) in collaboration with the United States and Britain.[^cite_note-407]

In August 2025, the [Australian Federal Police](341221.md) (AFP) charged a Chinese national and Australian permanent resident with foreign interference, alleging that she had covertly gathered information about the Canberra branch of the Buddhist association [Guan Yin Citta Dharma Door](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jun_Hong_Lu#As_a_Guan_Yin_Citta_Dharma_Door_leader), which is outlawed in China.[^cite_note-408]

In November 2025, ASIO director-general [Mike Burgess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Burgess_\(intelligence_chief\)) said hackers linked to the Chinese government and military had attempted to access Australia's critical infrastructure, including telecommunications networks. He identified the groups [Salt Typhoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_Typhoon) and [Volt Typhoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volt_Typhoon), which allegedly infiltrated U.S. systems for espionage and potential sabotage, and warned that similar probing had occurred in Australia. Burgess said Chinese officials had repeatedly complained to the Australian government and businesses about ASIO's public remarks on China, but he vowed to continue speaking out.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

In June 2026, the Australia and the rest of the [Five Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Eyes) issued a joint statement warning about Chinese espionage over online job platforms.[^cite_note-411]

#### New Zealand

See also: [Foreign espionage in New Zealand § China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_espionage_in_New_Zealand#China)

[Jian Yang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jian_Yang_\(politician\)), a member of the [New Zealand House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_House_of_Representatives) and the [New Zealand National Party](333149.md) was investigated by the [New Zealand Security Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Security_Intelligence_Service) (NZSIS) as a possibly spy due to his links to Chinese military and intelligence schools.[^cite_note-412] Yang reportedly failed to declare that he had taught at the [Air Force Engineering University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_Engineering_University) or the Luoyang People's Liberation Army University of Foreign Languages, which are commonly used as training grounds for Chinese intelligence officers.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] Yang has denied the allegations that he is a spy.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416]

In February 2020, the [Serious Fraud Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serious_Fraud_Office_\(New_Zealand\)) of New Zealand charged three Chinese nationals: Zhang Yikun, Zheng (Colin) Shijia, Zheng Hengjia and a member of parliament [Jami Lee Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jami-Lee_Ross) over allegations of providing misleading information in relation to donations to the National Party donations amounting up to \$100,000.[^cite_note-417] Zhang, a well known business man in the [New Zealand Chinese community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Zealanders) is a native of [Guangdong province](65109.md) and allegedly served in the People's Liberation Army prior to [immigrating to New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_New_Zealand) in 2000[^cite_note-\:0-418] as well as the founder of Chao San General Association (CGSA) ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): 新西兰潮属总会; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Xīnxīlán cháo shǔ zǒng huì*) registered with the Ministry of Ethnic Communities New Zealand.[^cite_note-419]

The stated purpose of the association is to serve the [Teochow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaozhou) community (an ethnic sub-group) from Guangdong in New Zealand, however Chinese dissident and local journalist Chen Wenjie has claimed the association is part of the CCP's United Front Work Department (UFWD) and donations by the association including those to the Christchurch Foundation in the sum of some \$2.1 million to assist the victims of the [2019 Mosque shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch_mosque_shootings) are part of a coordinated strategy to: "purchase political influence" and engage in "strategic infiltration" of the political systems of host countries.[^cite_note-420]

Local media outlet [Stuff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuff_\(website\)) also reported that Zhang led a delegation of New Zealand business leaders and politicians to visit the [Overseas Chinese Affairs Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Chinese_Affairs_Office) (OCAO) of the State Council, the Chinese government agency responsible for liaison with overseas Chinese communities in 2017 and that in 2018 the association officially became part of the UFWD.[^cite_note-\:0-418] On 25 February 2020, the Serious Fraud Office reported that Zhang along with three others had been charged with obtaining by deception under the Crimes Act and providing false or misleading information under the Serious Fraud Office Act, with each pleading not guilty in Auckland District Court.[^cite_note-421]

On 23 July 2020 local media outlet *newsroom* reported that two Chinese dissidents: Yuezhong Wang and Weiguo Xi had been killed in a car crash on their way to [Wellington](33804.md) to parliament to deliver a petition the [New Zealand Government](8899161.md) about Chinese Communist Party political interference in New Zealand.[^cite_note-422] Xi was reportedly a former member of the PLA while in China who campaigned against government corruption and was detained by police as a result of his activism and the Chairman of the New Zealand Branch of The Federation for a Democratic China; with being a writer affiliated with the [Chinese Democracy Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Democracy_Movement).[^cite_note-423] While no comments were made by police in relation to the circumstances of death were suspicious, according to political researcher [Anne-Marie Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne-Marie_Brady), significant speculation existed within the local Chinese diaspora community on whether Wang and Xi's death were the product of "sabotage".[^cite_note-424]

On 15 September 2020, the [Stuff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuff_\(company\)) media company reported that the Chinese data intelligence company [Zhenhua Data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhenhua_Data) had collected open source data intelligence on 730 New Zealand politicians, diplomats, academics, business executives, sportspersons, judges, fraudsters and their families including [Prime Minister](106321.md) [Jacinda Ardern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacinda_Ardern), former Prime Minister [John Key](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Key)'s son Max, former [Finance Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_\(New_Zealand\)) [Ruth Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Richardson), and sportsperson [Barbara Kendall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Kendall). Ten percent of Zhenhua Data's database had been leaked to American academic Chris Balding, who then passed the material to Canberra-based cybersecurity firm Internet 2.0.[^cite_note-425]

In August 2023, an NZSIS threat assessment which identified China, Iran, and Russia as the three foreign governments most responsible for foreign interference in New Zealand. According to the report, Chinese intelligence services were actively targeting ethnic Chinese communities in New Zealand including surveillance, monitoring, harassment, and threats of dissidents.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

In July 2024, [New Zealand Prime Minister](106321.md) [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) stated that New Zealand would disclose cases of Chinese espionage in the country to boost awareness of the security threat.[^cite_note-428] In September 2024, NZSIS stated in its second annual threat report that "China remains a complex intelligence concern."[^cite_note-429] The report said that China used professional social network websites such as [LinkedIn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LinkedIn) for intelligence gathering.[^cite_note-430]

On 21 August 2025, the NZSIS' third threat assessment expressed concern about Chinese [united front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_front) activities, which included using co-optees or proxies to carry out online and physical surveillance activities such as monitoring social media, photographing individuals, collecting information and taking control of community organisations. The NZSIS report also warned local councils that [sister city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_city) relationships could be exploited for "foreign interference" activities.[^cite_note-431] In response, local Chinese community leaders including Richard Leung and New Zealand Chinese Association president Paul Chin welcomed the NZSIS report but warned against stigmatising [Chinese New Zealanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Zealanders). While the Chinese Embassy condemned the report, [University of Canterbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Canterbury) political scientist Dr [Anne-Marie Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne-Marie_Brady) welcomed it for highlighting the national and external security threats facing New Zealand.[^cite_note-432]

In June 2026, the NZSIS and its Five Eyes partners warned that Chinese military intelligence operatives had been using job websites such as LinkedIn to trick people with access to national security clearances into divulging classified government and military information. This online recruitment strategy involved advertising fake freelance foreign policy and defence analyst jobs, and then coercing successful candidates into providing sensitive information. NZSIS director general Andrew Hampton confirmed that his agency had investigated several cases in the last three to four years, and prevented sensitive information from being passed over.[^cite_note-433] The Chinese Embassy to New Zealand denounced the alliance's warning as a "fabricated" smear campaign against China and warned New Zealand to refrain from "blindly following its security partners."[^cite_note-434] The Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson [Mao Ning](71679066.md) also denied the allegations, adding that the Five Eyes engaged in "massive systematic espionage" worldwide.[^cite_note-435]

In August 2026, it was reported that NZSIS disrupted an attempt by China's [Purple Mountain Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_Mountain_Observatory) to install satellite-tracking equipment in the country through a local company to collect information that could have been useful to the PLA.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

### Latin America

#### Argentina

This paragraph is an excerpt from [Espacio Lejano Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espacio_Lejano_Station).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Espacio_Lejano_Station\&action=edit)]

The [Espacio Lejano Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espacio_Lejano_Station) is a radio station located in [Loncopué Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loncopu%C3%A9_Department), [Neuquén Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuqu%C3%A9n_Province), Argentina, and is operated by the [Chinese National Space Administration](344618.md) as part of the [Chinese Deep Space Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Deep_Space_Network), in collaboration with [Argentina](18951905.md)'s [National Space Activities Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comisi%C3%B3n_Nacional_de_Actividades_Espaciales) (CONAE).[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-Espacio_Lejano_Station_\:0-439] The Chinese Deep Space Network is managed by the China Satellite Launch and Tracking Control General (CLTC), which was under the [People's Liberation Army Strategic Support Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Strategic_Support_Force)[^cite_note-Espacio_Lejano_Station_\:2-440][^cite_note-441] until its functions were transferred to the [People's Liberation Army Aerospace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Aerospace_Force) in 2024.

#### Chile

This section is an excerpt from [Chile–China relations § Cerro Ventarrones observatory](28899176.md#Cerro_Ventarrones_observatory).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chile%E2%80%93China_relations\&action=edit)]

In 2023, the [Catholic University of the North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_University_of_the_North) and the [National Astronomical Observatories of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Astronomical_Observatories_of_China) signed an agreement to develop an astronomical observatory in Cerro Ventarrones, [Antofagasta Region](72842.md).[^cite_note-Chile–China_relations_\:0-442][^cite_note-Chile–China_relations_\:1-443] In April 2025, the Chilean government froze development of the project.[^cite_note-Chile–China_relations_\:1-443][^cite_note-444] The government's action followed reporting on the lack of transparency around it and potential [military intelligence](146678.md) uses by the [People's Liberation Army](66890.md).[^cite_note-Chile–China_relations_\:0-442][^cite_note-445]

#### Cuba

Further information: [China–Cuba relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Cuba_relations) and [Bejucal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bejucal)

In June 2023, Cuba agreed to host a Chinese spying facility that could allow the Chinese to eavesdrop on electronic communications across the southeastern United States, an area populated with key military installations and extensive maritime traffic.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]

China has built [listening stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Listening_station) in [Bejucal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bejucal) and elsewhere on the island that can be used to monitor U.S. communications.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]

#### Peru

The computer security firm [ESET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESET) reported that tens of thousands of [blueprints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueprints) were stolen from Peruvian corporations through [malware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malware), which were traced to Chinese e-mail accounts. This was done through an [AutoCAD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoCAD) [worm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_worm) called ACAD/Medre.A, written in [AutoLISP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoLISP), which located AutoCAD files, at which point they were sent to QQ and [163.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/163.com) email accounts in China.[^cite_note-452] ESET researcher Righard Zwienenberg claimed this was Chinese industrial espionage.[^cite_note-453] The virus was mostly localized to Peru but spread to a few neighboring countries before being contained.[^cite_note-454]

#### Venezuela

Further information: [China–Venezuela relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Venezuela_relations)

China operates two satellite tracking stations in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:42-455] Chinese state-owned [China Great Wall Industry Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Great_Wall_Industry_Corporation) built the El Sombrero satellite tracking station at the [Captain Manuel Ríos Aerospace Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_Manuel_R%C3%ADos_Aerospace_Base) and another at Luepa, [Bolívar](556256.md).[^cite_note-\:42-455]

[^cite_note-226]: &#x20;["European media repeatedly fabricate 'Chinese espionage'"](https://web.archive.org/web/20160112120751/http://en.people.cn/200507/01/eng20050701_193539.html). *[People's Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Daily)*. 1 July 2005. Some European countries deliberately sensationalized the so-called 'Chinese espionage' in the past half a month. At the end of April, France set off a new upsurge of 'Chinese girl student as industrial espionage'; on 9 May, various leading media in Sweden followed suit by creating the Karolinska 'Chinese scholar espionage'; on 11 May, L'Agence France-Presse and a Belgian news website concocted a 'Chinese economic espionage website' at Katholieke Universiteit Leuven. For a second, the 'Chinese espionage' incidents had spread like wildfire in the European continent. \[...] When asked the reason for these false reports, Sun Ling, Counselor for Education of Chinese Embassy in Sweden, noted that the fast development of China's economy made a few people who hold biased attitude towards China feel ill at ease. They think that China's rapid development was achieved through illegal means such as grabbing advanced techniques from the Western countries. If we have a look at the latest development of such incidents, the truth will be clear that such moves are a vicious undercurrent discriminating and demonizing China.
